In essence, the perception of opportunities is linked to the life of the four freedoms, the four fundamental freedoms. If we make restrictions in the area of freedom of employment, in the field of services, this is also a restriction of freedoms. So we may be taking away something in this area, which is the overall objective. Therefore, it can only be associated with the prevention of uncertainties if we set seven years of transitional periods here. I am personally in favour of having transitional periods, because they take fears and emotions and because this should be a gradual transition from the Is-Time point at a time in seven years. But you may have observed that other countries also had transitional periods during the accession period, such as Spain and Portugal, of ten years. What have they observed about the transitional periods? Five years! For us, the automatic system is not related to seven years, but to two years, then a report follows; it is then possible for three more years and then, if there are really problems in the labour market, two more years. So we're probably not going to need these seven years. I am also involved in a conceptual problem. I'm not quite happy with what we decide to do. Why? – ‘transition’ would mean that we gradually absorb the over-potential potential, the oversupply that might arise in the labour market in Austria, and gradually put in place measures to reduce this. What we do, however, does not happen step by step, but we continue de facto status quo 1 : 1. The status quo, 1 : 1 continued, probably presents us with problems, because then the company's location decisions probably do not go towards Austria, but towards other countries. Whoever read the “Economic Sheet” yesterday may also have read what Siemens says: We will invest accordingly in the East. – Trade delegates from different countries gave a press conference in Brussels and said: The labour rules could be a shot in their own knees, because instead of the workers coming to us, the businesses will probably go abroad. In my opinion, we should be careful and consider the following: If we could perhaps do this a little more unbureaucratically, could we not take the form step by step? – For this we have made various proposals by the Chamber of Commerce in the review, not because we believe that we do not need transitional periods, but because we have to live up to the transitional periods in practice. In my opinion, we do not need an indefinite transitional period, but there would have been two years, then we look in practice at what is actually happening, and then we continue qualitatively. What I believe to continue with “qualitative” is that we are finally concluding the border agreement with the Czech Republic, building a similar agreement with Slovakia, and that we are dealing with second-level executives in the business sector differently from up to now and much more unbureaucratically. That is what we will need in the future. If you say – and I understand the fears – that the labour market is currently in a rather difficult situation and is problematic, then you must see that you have a differentiated labour market. In the border area, there is not enough labour in the wood industry, in the meat industry, in the construction industry, in tourism. So that you also get the fear accordingly, that then everyone who comes is treated as second-class employees and our people competes: That's not true! We have long decided the AVRAG, and it says that everyone who comes to us needs to be rewarded accordingly according to the level here, at this level and on this basis. That means in the final effect, not destruction at some point, but step by step – that is what I think we should make stronger. We now have a transitional arrangement, and I hope that we will also live it quite pragmatically. We will grow in our economy, when it grows – it must also be said: it will hopefully grow in the next few years – and then the labour force will also need it.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-24-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00055_d7e9708	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! Today and tomorrow, we are discussing numerous European issues and EU targets that need to be implemented and adapted. This item is a change in the law on working time and working time, which the EU Working Time Directive also implements for workers in transport companies. This unanimous implementation to achieve an EU-compliant legal situation concerns workers in railway, tram, cable car, inland waterway and maritime companies as well as airport and air navigation staff. I am pleased that the Austrian legal situation is in principle already in line with EU law and that only minor legal changes are required to adapt. There is therefore not much to discuss for us, but it is close to taking a short position, as this issue is very important. One of the amendments to the law on working time and work rest relates to the definition of minimum daily rest periods, as well as their reduction and division possibilities. I would like to take advantage of the opportunity to point out the enormous social and family effects of rest periods and working breaks and how important it is that we create framework conditions that regulate and respect precisely these effects and demands through legal requirements. I believe that it is important in all areas of our working life that our employees have enough time for their families and social interests. In this context, I would like to mention the constantly debated work-free Sunday. “On the seventh day you will rest!” – we already know that from the Bible. Without a common day of rest, social life and especially family life would be at risk. It was also hardly possible to organize the numerous festivals and events, as all people had different leisure activities. In Upper Austria, for example, the labour-free Sunday is anchored in the state constitution. [[Abg. Mandak: This also concerns shop opening times, not only the work-free Sunday!]] Unfortunately, this is not possible in Vienna. The work-free Sunday is a prerequisite for many common cultural, religious and family activities. These invaluable ideal values cannot be evaluated with money. We of the ÖVP will also ensure that the pension provisions for Sundays and holidays are maintained in the future, or that workers are made friendly. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] [[Abg. Mandak: What about the shop opening times?]] Only around 15 percent of the employees in Austria are excluded from the ban on Sunday work, who, by their service, ensure our health, safety and quality of weekend and leisure time: Health professionals, safety executives, transport managers, farmers and gardeners, employees in tourism, gastronomy and cultural establishments. We owe these people to the fact that most of the population can rest and relax on the weekend and, in addition, enjoy all the comforts of life. [[Abg. Mandak: But you have completely failed to do so with the Commercial Regulation, Mr. Cot!]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey,]] At this point I would like to thank all those who also stand on Sundays and holidays in their job very much for these excellent achievements. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Nevertheless, a certain degree of flexibility in working hours is important for the economy and workers. I am especially in times of EU enlargement to liberalise working time in order to strengthen the economic location and at the same time to create and maintain jobs, to meet the interests of consumers according to shopping opportunities and to prevent possible flow of purchasing power abroad. But I am in favour of moderate liberalisation. The regulations in the law on working time and working time must be designed in a friendly manner. Sunday must remain an exception, because a business will only be successful if the employees feel comfortable. As we are currently talking about working conditions, legal framework conditions and their negotiations, I would like to say once again and strongly that I would like to address the problem of young people's entry salaries. Just as the social partners have agreed to this retirement law, I appeal that in future social dialogues, more attention will be paid to the underprivileged salaries of our young workers. Especially our young people need the financial space and the opportunity to invest in the period of family creation and housing. First steps have already been taken in this area, especially in the public sector, and I would ask you to maintain this course. We by the young ÖVP will further strengthen our commitment to this issue. This legislative adjustment will continue a further step in the good employment policy of this Federal Government. Thank you for your approval.

The Basic Supply Agreement ensures the provision of aid and protection to aliens, in particular the basic provision of asylum seekers, through joint measures of the Federal and Länder relative to 60 : 40. This will raise the level of supply of this important group of persons to a humanitarian level in accordance with the requirements of the EU Directive, which came into force in February 2005. In this regard, we would like to thank the then head of Group III/J, the Council of Ministers Dr. Heide-Marie Fenzl, who, for years, has been negotiating with the countries and finally led these negotiations to an appropriate outcome. That is why we are pleased that we have succeeded in initiating the withdrawal of this Section 2(2) of the Federal Insurance Act in the Committee on the Interior by means of a self-employed request and by means of a joint request from SPÖ, ÖVP and FPÖ to remove these contradictions from the Federal Caring Act. A direct threat to Austria or Austrian interests is currently not obvious according to our situation. On question 2: The main basis of the entire international exchange of information with security authorities is the Police Cooperation Act. In addition, there are numerous bilateral and multilateral cooperation agreements and agreements. We have international cooperation since the 11th century. It was significantly improved in September 2001. Accordingly, the establishment of an EU early warning system is essential. I also voted in favour of better cooperation between intelligence services at European level. Question 3: The Madrid terrorist attacks have not changed the assessment of the risk situation, but have confirmed the already estimated threat to Europe. ==References== In September 2001, the signs of terrorist activities and attacks on European targets are being planned in some European countries. This assessment remains true. Question 4: In the course of the security coordination at the Federal Ministry of the Interior, the following measures were taken: For the United States and Great Britain, more precisely for those who are Americans or British, have been since 20. In March 2003, the highest security measures were taken and maintained. This also applies to the institutions of these countries. For institutions and groups of persons from Spain, Italy, France, Poland, Germany, Australia, Canada and Japan, security measures have been increased on the basis of the commitment of these countries and permanent protection has been established with appropriate accompanying measures. Permanent protection functions have been installed for the UN building, and OPEC, OPEC and OSCE are also under permanent surveillance. For the international Austrian airports, priority controls for departure aircraft were carried out on the 12th. ==References== “Sky Marshals” are increasingly used on Austrian airlines. Intensification of international cooperation has been initiated and implemented as part of the exchange of information with liaison officers and partner organisations. We have set up an appropriate information and coordination body, raise awareness of the border control bodies and put in place appropriate measures for the provision of information. Question 5: At the special meeting of the Council of the European Union on 19 November. In March, I addressed the following points concerning the fight against terrorism: better networking of data, e.g. in the area of Eurodac, VIS and others, the faster implementation of biometric data, the creation of joint investigation teams, improved networking in the information field, in particular the strengthening of existing structures and the establishment of a terrorist coordinator and a body in which the different information from the existing areas can be pooled. I have also proposed that we should address medium and long-term prospects such as the creation of a European intelligence service or a European Police Corps. On question 6: In general, all short-term proposals have been taken up by the Ministers of the European Union with the aim of implementing them. There was also the realization that we need new elements in the medium term. Furthermore, the already decided EU-terrorism coordinator should be mentioned, the establishment of a body entitled “Message Service Capacity”, the Commission to make proposals for the exchange of personal information by June 2004. Commission proposals for the inclusion of biometric features in passports and visas are to be submitted by the end of 2004. The education of joint investigation teams has already been implemented in Austria. The creation of a European intelligence service is already a step closer by the establishment of an EU-wide terrorism coordinator. The European Council also supports the key role of the United Nations and will continue to work to ensure that all resolutions of the Security Council, the Convention on Terrorism and the Protocols on this matter are fully signed and implemented. Question 7: The coordinator, who will carry out his activities within the Council Secretariat, will coordinate the work of the Council on combating terrorism and, taking into account the powers of the Commission, keep in mind all the instruments available to the Union in order to report regularly to the Council and to ensure effective action under the Council Decisions. Question 8: The coordinator will report regularly to the European Council and the Council for Justice and Home Affairs. On the question 9: Such structures are present. The acceding EU Member States have observer status in all EU bodies as well as in the Counter-Terrorism Group. Austria has made a further contribution through the formation of the Salzburg Group. We have extensive bilateral contacts in these countries, which are ensured through the Middle European Conference. Question 10: The European Council of 25 and 26. Thus, a close safety network has been set up to serve the population, enabling the identification and fight of germ cells of crime and terrorism as early as possible. Security for the people is at the forefront of this federal government. In order to be able to make effective security policy in terms of combating terrorism, it is necessary to strengthen international links. I myself have in my constituency a community where asylum seekers are housed in former apartments. In addition to concerns about people's safety, there is also fear of a decline in tourism. In order to counter this rejection, a number of innovations have also been decided in this area. Information, crisis and conflict management will be established between the care institutions and the municipalities. The number of Asylum seekers should not exceed 10 percent of the population, which I think is very important. There should be employment opportunities for asylum seekers. For me, it is particularly important for regulations to be found so that communities do not incur excessive costs in the care of children and old people. There are currently 16 000 persons to be cared for in Austria. The cost is about €125 million per year. 25 million of them are borne by the Federation. The remaining costs are borne by the countries relative to the population. The procedures will normally last only four months in the future. However, if they last longer than 12 months, the cost of accommodation will be borne by the federal government. It is also positive that there will no longer be any distinction between national and federal care. All aliens in need must be cared for to the same extent. The SPÖ heads of state have also agreed to the Article 15a agreement, thereby contributing to the Government template. I would ask you, ladies and gentlemen, to give your assent to this proposal. - Thank you.

[[Call the ÖVP:... call you!]] [[Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, ……]] Thank you. The EEA Agreement was concluded in Porto in 1992. At the time, even in the expert rounds, EEA was called a “European queue” because it should bring closer to the EU and join economically those EFTA countries that wished for a deeper EU integration but did not want to join or could not join directly. Much of the acquis had to be taken over by the Community, but without being able to take part – a point that we have repeatedly pointed out. In fact, the EEA was a boot spoon, a boot spoon to enter the European Union. It has also not taken long for Austria, Sweden and Finland to take this step, while Norway has been prevented by a referendum. – We are already at the point: Iceland, Norway and Liechtenstein are currently those three countries that belong to the EEA other than the EU States. More recently, the Treaty with the ten candidate countries for accession to the European Union agreed that these ten countries should also join the EEA, which is part of the Accession Treaty. Therefore, this Treaty must now be ratified by both EU members and these other three countries. It took a little while to find a solution. Two problems were to be solved. One problem was the question of fishing. – When Austria and Norway negotiated the famous four days and three nights in Brussels with Sweden and Finland at the time, EU experts have always said: What the transit for Austria is the fish for Norway! And so was that. It was certainly one of the reasons why this agreement, which had been negotiated by Norway, was rejected by the population. Finally, it has been possible to reach a solution in terms of fishing quotas and quotas for fish processing, which is particularly important for Poland and the Baltic States, but also for Iceland and Norway. Herrings and mackerel were counted and assigned exactly, the problem is solved. The second major problem was the question of contribution payments. The three states of Norway, Iceland and Liechtenstein are now paying higher contributions to the countries of the European Union, because they also have a larger economic area for their economies. This money will be spent mainly on security projects, on environmental projects and on regional development in the new candidate countries of the European Union, so it is certainly very well-designed there. It is very important that the new EU countries are also members of the EEA. This is also important for the future, for example Norway, if the people once have a different opinion, or if Iceland or perhaps even Liechtenstein can more easily join the European Union. Especially Liechtenstein – whose prince was present here today – had to reduce its free trade links with Switzerland considerably and tighten customs regulations, as Switzerland did not join the EEA. Switzerland has chosen the way to strengthen its links with the EU through seven special agreements, but does not want to be a member of the EEA. In this sense, I hope that the High House of this ratification today will agree that the EEA countries, together with the current EU countries and the ten newly acceding countries, will also be a strong European economic area. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2004-03-24-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00055_d7e18428	Mr President. High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The implementation of the reform of the common agricultural policy will undoubtedly be a central issue for us this year. What happened? What is the starting position? In view of the question of WTO negotiations at international level, we have had to give answers to this question in Europe, which is forgotten by a large number of people: How do we re-establish the agricultural system in order to counter the massive cuts from the US and other countries, such as Australia, New Zealand, CAIRNSruppe, in order to prevent a massive reduction in compensation in the European agricultural system? We have thus received a new discussion in Europe, namely on the question of decoupling premiums from production in response to overseas reduction proposals. That is why we have had a long and intense negotiation phase. As usual, on such topics, which are very complex, it was on 26 May. June 2003: We have to give new answers, we have to put forward predictable concepts for European farmers, for a period of up to 2013. I think that we – and this is the point to be considered – have been able to achieve blatant improvements in relation to the original Commission proposals. [[Abg. Dr. Pirklhuber: But also deterioration!]] We have achieved substantial improvements in almost all areas of production of European and, in particular, Austrian agriculture. This applies to milk, cattle, cereals, renewable raw materials on set-aside areas, a sector that has a lot of future and has not been adequately guaranteed in the original proposals. We have also given the right answers from the Austrian point of view to the overall decoupling originally planned by the Commission. You, Mr Pirklhuber, mentioned that there was a different opinion in the run-up, April 2003: Yes, we could also revise some! I would remind you that there is a Wifo study, in which it was pointed out that, in the case of a total decoupling model in the bovine sector, we are facing a massive problem of migration in the rural areas, especially in those areas that are particularly dear to me. In the mining zones I to IV, a total decoupling model on the issue of cattle premiums would have been massively damaging to us. What's the result? What are the answers to the implementation of the reform of the common agricultural policy? It is true that we can choose nationally from different models, but all these models actually have their advantages and disadvantages and we need to give the right answers to our structure. We will choose in Austria the full application of the total decoupling in the crop sector, with the uniform farm premium giving the right answers. It is not true that we did not provide for any regulation for those who had problems due to accidents during the observation period. On the contrary: There is a rigidity system that allows us to deal with those who had problems in their management during the historical period. We will be this single operating premium from 1st century onwards. Consistency of implementation in January 2005. We will link the mother cow premium to the animal at 100 percent – thus giving us the right answer for the mountain areas in order to maintain an important production in the alpine region – and let the slaughter premium be coupled up to 40 percent. Ladies and gentlemen! A debate on agricultural reform must not lead to massive redistribution. Why not? – Because, unlike some other countries in the European Union, we have developed a system in Austria in recent years, especially since 1995 before accession to the European Union, in addition to the market regulations, with a strong emphasis on rural development, namely for the mining industry in Zone IV, but also for producers in the favour or intensive conditions of the East. With our national programmes – rural development, environmental programmes, investment programmes – we have responded very, very balancedly to disadvantages or benefits. And it makes no sense to bring this balanced system into a wrong position through a redistribution debate. We have to give correct answers! And the question of decoupling at historical time is the right answer. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] How will we implement this? . We were able to achieve great negotiation success in rural development – which underlines the way of Austria once again: More than 50,000 suckler cow premiums for Austria, also a response to the total linkage, bringing €10 million per year to the farmers. By forcing rural development, with modulation – 5 percent, over €5,000 – we can achieve a strengthening of the second pillar of rural development. 144 million € until 2013, a plus in the balance. We will use this money specifically and strategicly for rural development, for those farms that are planning to invest in the future, which are expanding those who are looking for new feet, which diversify – and do what is characterized by Austrian agriculture, namely, to enter into competition. Strengthening investment in rural development is the right idea for the future. Ladies and gentlemen! You see, the concept is coherent. We have discussed this extensively: in the subcommittee, in the committees. We have discussed very intensively the orientation and the answers from the reform of the common agricultural policy. [[Abg. But that can only be a joke! Where did we discuss this?]] I believe that the national implementation of the reform will give the right answers to a rural, land-based, ecologically-oriented agriculture with eco-social foundations in Austria. In this sense, we are on the right path. We have the right answers and right concepts, and this will ultimately strengthen the rural family farms in Austria.

However, the Constitutional Court has now found that a provision of the Waste Management Act was unconstitutional. Thus, when assessing whether certain landfills are from the prohibition of waste disposal up to a maximum of 31. It is not the form of the Regulation, but only a decision-making procedure, which is to be exempted on December 2008. The reason for this is that, in this assessment, mainly individual criteria of the individual landfills are to be examined whether, for example, they are suitable for waste with more than 5 % by weight of total carbon, thus invalidating the form of the regulation. The proposal now before us is intended to eliminate this illegality of the Constitution. However, the planned implementation of the federal states on non-hazardous waste disposal facilities must continue to be made possible in the process of regulation, but at the same time take into account the objections of the Constitutional Court. This will increase the degree of planning security for the federal states by allowing, on the one hand, existing projects of the countries relating to waste disposal plants by means of a regulation, for which it would be necessary, according to the Constitutional Court of Justice, to take account of the criticism of the Constitutional Court. The existing regulations on the proper disposal of waste with more than 5 % by mass of total carbon in all federal states are now intended for a short period of time, i.e. until 31. ==References====External links== In any case, the change of law does not mean the requirements of the federal states, which are already in force from 1 January. However, in the past, operators of pre-treatment facilities such as incinerators have already made investment decisions on the grounds of post-care and precautionary measures. This proposal again helps to underline the country's self-determination and strengthen the countries. An important step towards more federalism, as we need it in Austria.
